Loading and Initial Inspection\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\n\n# Load data\ndf = pd.read_csv('data.csv')\n\n# Initial inspection\nprint(df.shape)\nprint(df.dtypes)\nprint(df.head())\nprint(df.describe())\nprint(df.isnull().sum())\n```\n\n### 2. Handling Duplicates\n```python\n# Check for duplicates\nduplicates = df.duplicated()\nprint(f\"Number of duplicates: {duplicates.sum()}\")\n\n# Remove duplicates (keep first occurrence)\ndf_clean = df.drop_duplicates(keep='first')\n\n# Remove duplicates based on specific columns\ndf_clean = df.drop_duplicates(subset=['id_column'], keep='first')\n\n# Identify duplicate rows for inspection\nduplicate_rows = df[df.duplicated(keep=False)]\n```\n\n### 3. Handling Missing Values\n```python\n# Identify missing values\nmissing_counts = df.isnull().sum()\nmissing_pct = (df.isnull().sum() / len(df)) * 100\n\n# Drop rows with any missing values\ndf_clean = df.dropna()\n\n# Drop rows where specific columns are missing\ndf_clean = df.dropna(subset=['important_col'])\n\n# Fill missing values\ndf['col'] = df['col'].fillna(df['col'].median())  # numerical\ndf['col'] = df['col'].fillna(df['col'].mode()[0])  # categorical\ndf['col'] = df['col'].fillna('Unknown')  # constant value\n\n# Forward/backward fill for time series\ndf['col'] = df['col'].fillna(method='ffill')\ndf['col'] = df['col'].fillna(method='bfill')\n```\n\n### 4. Data Type Conversion\n```python\n# Convert to datetime\ndf['date'] = pd.to_datetime(df['date'], format='%Y-%m-%d', errors='coerce')\n\n# Convert to numeric (handling non-numeric values)\ndf['price'] = pd.to_numeric(df['price'].str.replace('$', '').str.replace(',', ''), errors='coerce')\n\n# Convert to categorical\ndf['category'] = df['category'].astype('category')\n\n# Convert boolean-like strings\ndf['bool_col'] = df['bool_col'].map({'Yes': True, 'No': False, 'yes': True, 'no': False})\n```\n\n### 5. String Cleaning\n```python\n# Strip whitespace\ndf['col'] = df['col'].str.strip()\n\n# Standardize case\ndf['col'] = df['col'].str.lower()\ndf['col'] = df['col'].str.title()\n\n# Remove special characters\ndf['col'] = df['col'].str.replace(r'[^\\w\\s]', '', regex=True)\n\n# Handle inconsistent values\ndf['col'] = df['col'].replace({'old_value1': 'new_value', 'old_value2': 'new_value'})\n```\n\n### 6. Handling Outliers\n```python\n# IQR method\nQ1 = df['col'].quantile(0.25)\nQ3 = df['col'].quantile(0.75)\nIQR = Q3 - Q1\nlower_bound = Q1 - 1.5 * IQR\nupper_bound = Q3 + 1.5 * IQR\ndf_clean = df[(df['col'] >= lower_bound) & (df['col'] <= upper_bound)]\n\n# Z-score method\nfrom scipy import stats\ndf['z_score'] = np.abs(stats.zscore(df['col']))\ndf_clean = df[df['z_score'] < 3]\n```\n\n### 7. Validating Data Ranges\n```python\n# Check for invalid values\ninvalid_prices = df[df['price'] < 0]\ninvalid_dates = df[df['date'] > pd.Timestamp.now()]\n\n# Filter valid ranges\ndf_clean = df[(df['price'] >= 0) & (df['quantity'] > 0)]\n```\n\n### 8. Statistical Methods for Anomaly Detection\n\n#### Z-Score Method\n```python\nfrom scipy import stats\n\ndef detect_anomalies_zscore(series, threshold=3):\n    \"\"\"Detect anomalies using z-score method\"\"\"\n    z_scores = np.abs(stats.zscore(series))\n    return z_scores > threshold\n```\n\n#### Rolling Statistics\n```python\ndef detect_anomalies_rolling(series, window=30, n_std=2):\n    \"\"\"Detect anomalies using rolling mean and std\"\"\"\n    rolling_mean = series.rolling(window=window).mean()\n    rolling_std = series.rolling(window=window).std()\n\n    upper_bound = rolling_mean + n_std * rolling_std\n    lower_bound = rolling_mean - n_std * rolling_std\n\n    anomalies = (series > upper_bound) | (series < lower_bound)\n    return anomalies, upper_bound, lower_bound\n```\n\n### 3. Counterfactual Prediction\n\n#### Using Historical Average\n```python\ndef counterfactual_historical_avg(pre_period_data, post_period_length):\n    \"\"\"Predict counterfactual using historical average\"\"\"\n    return np.repeat(pre_period_data.mean(), post_period_length)\n```\n\n#### Using Same Period Last Year\n```python\ndef counterfactual_same_period_last_year(df, target_start, target_end):\n    \"\"\"Use same period from previous year as counterfactual\"\"\"\n    last_year_start = target_start - pd.DateOffset(years=1)\n    last_year_end = target_end - pd.DateOffset(years=1)\n    return df[(df['date'] >= last_year_start) & (df['date'] <= last_year_end)]\n```\n\n#### Using Prophet for Counterfactual\n```python\nfrom prophet import Prophet\n\ndef predict_counterfactual_prophet(df, pre_period_end, prediction_periods):\n    \"\"\"\n    Train on pre-period data and predict counterfactual for post-period\n    \"\"\"\n    # Prepare data for Prophet\n    train_df = df[df['ds'] <= pre_period_end][['ds', 'y']]\n\n    # Fit model\n    model = Prophet(yearly_seasonality=True, weekly_seasonality=True, daily_seasonality=False)\n    model.fit(train_df)\n\n    # Make future predictions\n    future = model.make_future_dataframe(periods=prediction_periods)\n    forecast = model.predict(future)\n\n    return forecast[['ds', 'yhat', 'yhat_lower', 'yhat_upper']]\n```\n\n### 4. Anomaly Index Calculation\n\n```python\ndef calculate_anomaly_index(actual, predicted, method='normalized'):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate anomaly index ranging from -100 to 100\n\n    100: Maximum positive anomaly (surge)\n    0: No anomaly (actual = predicted)\n    -100: Maximum negative anomaly (slump)\n    \"\"\"\n    if method == 'normalized':\n        # Calculate percentage deviation\n        deviation = (actual - predicted) / (predicted + 1e-10)  # avoid division by zero\n\n        # Normalize to [-100, 100] using tanh\n        anomaly_index = np.tanh(deviation) * 100\n\n    elif method == 'zscore':\n        # Use z-score based approach\n        combined = np.concatenate([actual, predicted])\n        std = np.std(combined) if np.std(combined) > 0 else 1\n        z_score = (actual.mean() - predicted.mean()) / std\n        anomaly_index = np.tanh(z_score) * 100\n\n    return anomaly_index\n```\n\n### 5. Category-Level Anomaly Detection\n\n```python\ndef analyze_category_anomalies(df, baseline_start, baseline_end,\n                               treatment_start, treatment_end):\n    \"\"\"\n    Analyze anomalies for each product category\n    \"\"\"\n    results = []\n\n    for category in df['category'].unique():\n        cat_data = df[df['category'] == category].copy()\n\n        # Get baseline period data (all historical data before treatment)\n        baseline = cat_data[(cat_data['date'] >= baseline_start) &\n                           (cat_data['date'] <= baseline_end)]\n\n        # Get treatment period data\n        treatment = cat_data[(cat_data['date'] >= treatment_start) &\n                            (cat_data['date'] <= treatment_end)]\n\n        if len(baseline) > 0 and len(treatment) > 0:\n            # Calculate predicted (counterfactual) from baseline\n            predicted_daily_avg = baseline['sales'].mean()\n            actual_daily_avg = treatment['sales'].mean()\n\n            # Calculate anomaly index\n            anomaly_idx = calculate_anomaly_index(\n                np.array([actual_daily_avg]),\n                np.array([predicted_daily_avg])\n            )\n\n            results.append({\n                'category': category,\n                'anomaly_index': anomaly_idx,\n                'baseline_avg': predicted_daily_avg,\n                'treatment_avg': actual_daily_avg\n            })\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(results)\n```\n\n### 6. Identifying Surges and Slumps\n\n```python\ndef identify_surges_slumps(anomaly_df, n_top=10):\n    \"\"\"\n    Identify top surge and slump categories\n    \"\"\"\n    # Sort by anomaly index\n    sorted_df = anomaly_df.sort_values('anomaly_index', ascending=False)\n\n    # Top surges (highest positive anomaly)\n    surges = sorted_df.head(n_top)\n\n    # Top slumps (lowest negative anomaly)\n    slumps = sorted_df.tail(n_top)\n\n    return surges, slumps\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n1. Use sufficient historical data for reliable predictions\n2. Account for seasonality (weekly, monthly, yearly patterns)\n3. Consider multiple baseline periods for robust estimates\n4. Binary Feature Extraction from Yes/No Questions\n```python\ndef encode_binary_response(series, yes_values=['Yes', 'yes', 'Y', 'y', '1', True]):\n    \"\"\"Convert yes/no responses to binary 0/1\"\"\"\n    return series.isin(yes_values).astype(int)\n\n# Apply to multiple columns\nbinary_cols = ['Q-personal-diabetes', 'Q-personal-wheelchair']\nfor col in binary_cols:\n    new_col = col.replace('Q-', 'has_').replace('-', '_')\n    df[new_col] = encode_binary_response(df[col])\n```\n\n### 4. Handling Multi-Select Responses\n```python\ndef encode_multiselect(series, separator=','):\n    \"\"\"Encode multi-select responses into binary columns\"\"\"\n    # Split and get unique values\n    all_values = series.str.split(separator).explode().str.strip().unique()\n    all_values = [v for v in all_values if pd.notna(v)]\n\n    # Create binary columns for each value\n    result = pd.DataFrame()\n    for value in all_values:\n        col_name = f\"has_{value.replace(' ', '_').lower()}\"\n        result[col_name] = series.str.contains(value, na=False).astype(int)\n\n    return result\n```\n\n### 5. 24 years': 'GenZ',\n        '25 - 34 years': 'Millennial',\n        '35 - 44 years': 'Millennial',\n        '45 - 54 years': 'GenX',\n        '55 - 64 years': 'Boomer',\n        '65 and older': 'Boomer'\n    }\n    df['generation'] = df[age_col].map(gen_map)\n\n    return df\n```\n\n#### Income Features\n```python\ndef create_income_features(df, income_col):\n    \"\"\"Create derived income features\"\"\"\n    # Map to numeric midpoints\n    income_map = {\n        'Less than $25,000': 12500,\n        '$25,000 - $49,999': 37500,\n        '$50,000 - $74,999': 62500,\n        '$75,000 - $99,999': 87500,\n        '$100,000 - $149,999': 125000,\n        '$150,000 or more': 175000,\n        'Prefer not to say': None\n    }\n    df['income_midpoint'] = df[income_col].map(income_map)\n\n    # Create income brackets\n    def income_bracket(val):\n        if val in ['Less than $25,000']:\n            return 'low_income'\n        elif val in ['$25,000 - $49,999', '$50,000 - $74,999']:\n            return 'middle_income'\n        else:\n            return 'high_income'\n\n    df['income_bracket'] = df[income_col].apply(income_bracket)\n\n    return df\n```\n\n#### Household Size Features\n```python\ndef create_household_features(df, hh_col, users_col):\n    \"\"\"Create household-related features\"\"\"\n    # Parse numeric values\n    hh_map = {'1 (just me!)': 1, '2': 2, '3': 3, '4+': 4}\n    df['household_size'] = df[hh_col].map(hh_map)\n    df['amazon_users'] = df[users_col].map(hh_map)\n\n    # Create ratios\n    df['amazon_penetration'] = df['amazon_users'] / df['household_size']\n\n    # Create binary flags\n    df['is_single_household'] = (df['household_size'] == 1).astype(int)\n    df['is_large_household'] = (df['household_size'] >= 3).astype(int)\n\n    return df\n```\n\n### 6. Interaction Features\n```python\ndef create_interaction_features(df, col1, col2, prefix='interaction'):\n    \"\"\"Create interaction features between two categorical columns\"\"\"\n    df[f'{prefix}_{col1}_{col2}'] = df[col1].astype(str) + '_' + df[col2].astype(str)\n\n    # One-hot encode the interaction\n    interaction_dummies = pd.get_dummies(\n        df[f'{prefix}_{col1}_{col2}'],\n        prefix=f'{prefix}'\n    )\n    return pd.concat([df, interaction_dummies], axis=1)\n```\n\n### 7. Frequency Encoding\n```python\ndef frequency_encode(df, col):\n    \"\"\"Encode categories by their frequency\"\"\"\n    freq = df[col].value_counts(normalize=True)\n    df[f'{col}_freq'] = df[col].map(freq)\n    return df\n```\n\n### 8. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-5-20251101","id":"msg_vrtx_01WwbCm4xfruNnsbgnRVj2Xb","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01CcXnmJWPHxEe6kCDRvapjR","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/app/environment/skills/difference-in-differences-causal-analysis.md","content":"# Difference-in-Differences (DiD) Causal Analysis Skill\n\n## Overview\nThis skill covers the implementation of Difference-in-Differences analysis for causal inference, including intensive margin (spending amount) and extensive margin (participation) analysis with heterogeneous treatment effects.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install pandas numpy scipy statsmodels linearmodels\n```\n\n## Key Concepts\n\n### Intensive vs Extensive Margin\n- **Intensive Margin**: Changes in spending amount among those who purchase (how much more/less do buyers spend?)\n- **Extensive Margin**: Changes in purchase probability (are more/fewer people buying?)\n\n### DiD Estimator\nThe basic DiD estimator captures the treatment effect:\n```\nDiD = (Y_treatment,post - Y_treatment,pre) - (Y_control,post - Y_control,pre)\n```\n\nFor heterogeneous effects, we interact the DiD term with demographic features.\n\n## Key Techniques\n\n### 1. Data Preparation for DiD\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef prepare_did_data(purchases, demographics, baseline_start, baseline_end,\n                     treatment_start, treatment_end, categories):\n    \"\"\"\n    Prepare data for DiD analysis\n    \"\"\"\n    # Filter purchases to relevant categories and time periods\n    purchases = purchases[purchases['category'].isin(categories)]\n\n    # Create period indicator\n    purchases['period'] = np.where(\n        (purchases['date'] >= treatment_start) & (purchases['date'] <= treatment_end),\n        'treatment', 'baseline'\n    )\n    purchases = purchases[purchases['period'].isin(['treatment', 'baseline'])]\n\n    # Aggregate by user, category, period\n    agg_intensive = purchases.groupby(['user_id', 'category', 'period']).agg({\n        'amount': 'sum'\n    }).reset_index()\n    agg_intensive.columns = ['user_id', 'category', 'period', 'total_spend']\n\n    # For extensive margin, need to track who purchased\n    agg_extensive = purchases.groupby(['user_id', 'category', 'period']).size().reset_index()\n    agg_extensive.columns = ['user_id', 'category', 'period', 'n_purchases']\n    agg_extensive['has_purchase'] = 1\n\n    # Merge with demographics\n    agg_intensive = agg_intensive.merge(demographics, on='user_id')\n    agg_extensive = agg_extensive.merge(demographics, on='user_id')\n\n    return agg_intensive, agg_extensive\n```\n\n### 2. Univariate DiD for Intensive Margin\n```python\nimport statsmodels.api as sm\nfrom scipy import stats\n\ndef univariate_did_intensive(df, feature_col, spend_col='total_spend', period_col='period'):\n    \"\"\"\n    Run univariate DiD analysis for intensive margin\n\n    For each feature value, estimate how the treatment effect differs.\n    \"\"\"\n    results = []\n\n    # Get unique feature values\n    feature_values = df[feature_col].unique()\n\n    for feature_val in feature_values:\n        # Subset to this feature value\n        subset = df[df[feature_col] == feature_val]\n\n        # Calculate means\n        baseline_mean = subset[subset[period_col] == 'baseline'][spend_col].mean()\n        treatment_mean = subset[subset[period_col] == 'treatment'][spend_col].mean()\n\n        # DiD for this subgroup\n        did_estimate = treatment_mean - baseline_mean\n\n        results.append({\n            'feature': feature_col,\n            'feature_value': feature_val,\n            'baseline_mean': baseline_mean,\n            'treatment_mean': treatment_mean,\n            'did_estimate': did_estimate\n        })\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(results)\n\n\ndef did_with_interaction(df, feature_col, spend_col='total_spend'):\n    \"\"\"\n    DiD regression with feature interaction to identify heterogeneous effects\n\n    Y = beta0 + beta1*Post + beta2*Feature + beta3*(Post*Feature) + epsilon\n\n    beta3 captures the heterogeneous treatment effect\n    \"\"\"\n    # Create post period indicator (1 for treatment, 0 for baseline)\n    df = df.copy()\n    df['post'] = (df['period'] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n\n    # For binary features\n    if df[feature_col].nunique() == 2:\n        df['feature'] = df[feature_col].astype(int)\n    else:\n        # For categorical, use dummy coding\n        df['feature'] = pd.Categorical(df[feature_col]).codes\n\n    # Create interaction term\n    df['post_x_feature'] = df['post'] * df['feature']\n\n    # Run OLS regression\n    X = sm.add_constant(df[['post', 'feature', 'post_x_feature']])\n    y = df[spend_col]\n\n    model = sm.OLS(y, X).fit(cov_type='HC3')  # Robust standard errors\n\n    return {\n        'feature': feature_col,\n        'did_estimate': model.params['post_x_feature'],\n        'p_value': model.pvalues['post_x_feature'],\n        'std_err': model.bse['post_x_feature'],\n        'conf_int': model.conf_int().loc['post_x_feature'].tolist()\n    }\n```\n\n### 3. Extensive Margin DiD (Probability of Purchase)\n```python\ndef extensive_margin_did(df, feature_col, all_users_df, categories):\n    \"\"\"\n    Analyze extensive margin: probability of purchase\n\n    Need to include non-purchasers in the analysis\n    \"\"\"\n    results = []\n\n    for category in categories:\n        # Get all users who could have purchased\n        all_users = all_users_df.copy()\n\n        # Get purchasers for this category\n        cat_purchasers = df[df['category'] == category]\n\n        for period in ['baseline', 'treatment']:\n            period_purchasers = cat_purchasers[cat_purchasers['period'] == period]\n\n            # Create full panel with all users\n            all_users[f'purchased_{period}'] = all_users['user_id'].isin(\n                period_purchasers['user_id']\n            ).astype(int)\n\n        # Calculate purchase rates by feature\n        for feature_val in all_users[feature_col].unique():\n            subset = all_users[all_users[feature_col] == feature_val]\n\n            baseline_rate = subset['purchased_baseline'].mean()\n            treatment_rate = subset['purchased_treatment'].mean()\n\n            did_estimate = treatment_rate - baseline_rate\n\n            results.append({\n                'category': category,\n                'feature': feature_col,\n                'feature_value': feature_val,\n                'baseline_rate': baseline_rate,\n                'treatment_rate': treatment_rate,\n                'did_estimate': did_estimate\n            })\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(results)\n```\n\n### 4. Multivariate DiD with Heterogeneous Effects\n```python\ndef multivariate_did(df, feature_cols, outcome_col, is_binary_outcome=False):\n    \"\"\"\n    Multivariate DiD with multiple feature interactions\n\n    Y = beta0 + beta1*Post + sum(beta_k*Feature_k) + sum(gamma_k*Post*Feature_k) + epsilon\n    \"\"\"\n    df = df.copy()\n    df['post'] = (df['period'] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n\n    # Prepare features and interactions\n    X_vars = ['post']\n    for col in feature_cols:\n        X_vars.append(col)\n        interaction_col = f'post_x_{col}'\n        df[interaction_col] = df['post'] * df[col]\n        X_vars.append(interaction_col)\n\n    X = sm.add_constant(df[X_vars])\n    y = df[outcome_col]\n\n    if is_binary_outcome:\n        # Logistic regression for extensive margin\n        model = sm.Logit(y, X).fit(disp=0)\n    else:\n        # OLS for intensive margin\n        model = sm.OLS(y, X).fit(cov_type='HC3')\n\n    # Extract heterogeneous effects\n    results = []\n    for col in feature_cols:\n        interaction_col = f'post_x_{col}'\n        if interaction_col in model.params.index:\n            results.append({\n                'feature': col,\n                'did_estimate': model.params[interaction_col],\n                'p_value': model.pvalues[interaction_col],\n                'std_err': model.bse[interaction_col]\n            })\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(results), model\n```\n\n### 5. Identifying Top Drivers\n```python\ndef identify_top_drivers(did_results, n_top=3, margin_type='intensive'):\n    \"\"\"\n    Identify top features driving anomalies\n\n    For surges: rank by positive DiD estimates\n    For slumps: rank by negative DiD estimates\n    \"\"\"\n    # Sort by absolute DiD magnitude\n    did_results = did_results.copy()\n    did_results['abs_did'] = did_results['did_estimate'].abs()\n\n    # For surge categories (positive anomaly), want largest positive effects\n    surge_drivers = did_results.nlargest(n_top, 'did_estimate')\n\n    # For slump categories (negative anomaly), want largest negative effects\n    slump_drivers = did_results.nsmallest(n_top, 'did_estimate')\n\n    return surge_drivers, slump_drivers\n```\n\n### 6. Complete Analysis Pipeline\n```python\ndef run_causal_analysis(purchases, demographics, categories,\n                        baseline_period, treatment_period, feature_cols):\n    \"\"\"\n    Complete DiD analysis pipeline\n    \"\"\"\n    results = {\n        'surge_categories': [],\n        'slump_categories': []\n    }\n\n    for category in categories:\n        cat_results = {\n            'category': category,\n            'intensive_margin': [],\n            'extensive_margin': []\n        }\n\n        # Filter to category\n        cat_data = purchases[purchases['category'] == category]\n\n        # Intensive margin analysis\n        intensive_data = prepare_intensive_margin_data(cat_data, demographics)\n        for feature in feature_cols:\n            did_result = did_with_interaction(intensive_data, feature)\n            cat_results['intensive_margin'].append(did_result)\n\n        # Extensive margin analysis\n        extensive_data = prepare_extensive_margin_data(\n            cat_data, demographics, baseline_period, treatment_period\n        )\n        for feature in feature_cols:\n            did_result = extensive_margin_did_feature(extensive_data, feature)\n            cat_results['extensive_margin'].append(did_result)\n\n        # Sort and get top 3 drivers\n        cat_results['intensive_margin'] = sorted(\n            cat_results['intensive_margin'],\n            key=lambda x: abs(x['did_estimate']),\n            reverse=True\n        )[:3]\n\n        cat_results['extensive_margin'] = sorted(\n            cat_results['extensive_margin'],\n            key=lambda x: abs(x['did_estimate']),\n            reverse=True\n        )[:3]\n\n        results['surge_categories'].append(cat_results)\n\n    return results\n```\n\n## Assumptions and Validation\n1. **Parallel Trends**: Treatment and control groups would have followed similar trends without treatment\n2. **No Spillovers**: Treatment of one group doesn't affect outcomes of other groups\n3. **Stable Composition**: Group composition remains stable across periods\n\n## Best Practices\n1. Validate parallel trends assumption using pre-treatment data\n2. Use robust standard errors for inference\n3. Consider multiple comparison corrections when testing many features\n4. Simple Historical Average Approach\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef counterfactual_simple_avg(historical_data, target_period_length):\n    \"\"\"\n    Use simple historical average as counterfactual prediction\n    \"\"\"\n    avg_daily = historical_data.mean()\n    return np.full(target_period_length, avg_daily)\n```\n\n### 2. Same Period Prior Year\n```python\ndef counterfactual_prior_year(df, date_col, value_col,\n                               target_start, target_end):\n    \"\"\"\n    Use same calendar period from previous year\n    \"\"\"\n    # Calculate dates for previous year\n    prior_start = target_start - pd.DateOffset(years=1)\n    prior_end = target_end - pd.DateOffset(years=1)\n\n    # Get prior year data\n    prior_data = df[(df[date_col] >= prior_start) &\n                    (df[date_col] <= prior_end)][value_col]\n\n    return prior_data.values\n```\n\n### 3. Seasonal Decomposition + Trend Extrapolation\n```python\nfrom statsmodels.tsa.seasonal import seasonal_decompose\nfrom statsmodels.tsa.holtwinters import ExponentialSmoothing\n\ndef counterfactual_seasonal_decomposition(series, periods_ahead, freq=7):\n    \"\"\"\n    Decompose time series and extrapolate trend + seasonality\n    \"\"\"\n    # Decompose\n    decomposition = seasonal_decompose(series, model='additive', period=freq)\n\n    # Extrapolate trend using exponential smoothing\n    trend = decomposition.trend.dropna()\n    trend_model = ExponentialSmoothing(trend, trend='add').fit()\n    trend_forecast = trend_model.forecast(periods_ahead)\n\n    # Get seasonal pattern\n    seasonal = decomposition.seasonal\n    seasonal_pattern = seasonal.iloc[:freq].values\n\n    # Combine\n    n_periods = periods_ahead\n    seasonal_forecast = np.tile(seasonal_pattern, n_periods // freq + 1)[:n_periods]\n\n    counterfactual = trend_forecast.values + seasonal_forecast\n\n    return counterfactual\n```\n\n### 4. Prophet-Based Counterfactual\n```python\nfrom prophet import Prophet\n\ndef counterfactual_prophet(df, date_col, value_col,\n                           train_end, forecast_days):\n    \"\"\"\n    Use Prophet for sophisticated counterfactual prediction\n    \"\"\"\n    # Prepare data for Prophet\n    prophet_df = df[[date_col, value_col]].copy()\n    prophet_df.columns = ['ds', 'y']\n    prophet_df = prophet_df[prophet_df['ds'] <= train_end]\n\n    # Fit model\n    model = Prophet(\n        yearly_seasonality=True,\n        weekly_seasonality=True,\n        daily_seasonality=False,\n        changepoint_prior_scale=0.05  # Control flexibility\n    )\n    model.fit(prophet_df)\n\n    # Create future dataframe\n    future = model.make_future_dataframe(periods=forecast_days)\n    forecast = model.predict(future)\n\n    # Get counterfactual predictions\n    counterfactual = forecast[forecast['ds'] > train_end][['ds', 'yhat', 'yhat_lower', 'yhat_upper']]\n\n    return counterfactual\n```\n\n### 5. Regression-Based Counterfactual\n```python\nimport statsmodels.api as sm\n\ndef counterfactual_regression(df, date_col, value_col,\n                              train_end, target_dates):\n    \"\"\"\n    Use regression with time features for counterfactual\n    \"\"\"\n    df = df.copy()\n\n    # Create time features\n    df['day_of_week'] = pd.to_datetime(df[date_col]).dt.dayofweek\n    df['month'] = pd.to_datetime(df[date_col]).dt.month\n    df['trend'] = range(len(df))\n\n    # Split data\n    train = df[df[date_col] <= train_end]\n\n    # Create dummies for categorical features\n    X_train = pd.get_dummies(train[['trend', 'day_of_week', 'month']],\n                             columns=['day_of_week', 'month'])\n    X_train = sm.add_constant(X_train)\n    y_train = train[value_col]\n\n    # Fit model\n    model = sm.OLS(y_train, X_train).fit()\n\n    # Prepare prediction features\n    pred_df = pd.DataFrame({'date': target_dates})\n    pred_df['day_of_week'] = pred_df['date'].dt.dayofweek\n    pred_df['month'] = pred_df['date'].dt.month\n    pred_df['trend'] = range(len(train), len(train) + len(target_dates))\n\n    X_pred = pd.get_dummies(pred_df[['trend', 'day_of_week', 'month']],\n                            columns=['day_of_week', 'month'])\n    X_pred = sm.add_constant(X_pred)\n\n    # Ensure same columns\n    for col in X_train.columns:\n        if col not in X_pred.columns:\n            X_pred[col] = 0\n    X_pred = X_pred[X_train.columns]\n\n    # Predict\n    counterfactual = model.predict(X_pred)\n\n    return counterfactual\n```\n\n### 6. Anomaly Score Calculation\n```python\ndef calculate_anomaly_score(actual_values, counterfactual_values,\n                            method='tanh_normalized', scale=1.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate anomaly score ranging from -100 to 100\n\n    Parameters:\n    - actual_values: observed values during treatment period\n    - counterfactual_values: predicted values (what should have happened)\n    - method: scoring method\n    - scale: scaling factor for sensitivity\n\n    Returns:\n    - anomaly_score: value in [-100, 100]\n      - 100: maximum surge (actual >> counterfactual)\n      - 0: no anomaly (actual ≈ counterfactual)\n      - -100: maximum slump (actual << counterfactual)\n    \"\"\"\n    actual_mean = np.mean(actual_values)\n    cf_mean = np.mean(counterfactual_values)\n\n    if method == 'tanh_normalized':\n        # Calculate relative deviation\n        if cf_mean > 0:\n            relative_dev = (actual_mean - cf_mean) / cf_mean\n        else:\n            relative_dev = actual_mean - cf_mean\n\n        # Apply tanh for bounded output\n        anomaly_score = np.tanh(relative_dev * scale) * 100\n\n    elif method == 'zscore':\n        # Z-score based approach\n        cf_std = np.std(counterfactual_values)\n        if cf_std > 0:\n            z = (actual_mean - cf_mean) / cf_std\n        else:\n            z = 0\n        anomaly_score = np.tanh(z * scale) * 100\n\n    elif method == 'percentile':\n        # Percentile-based (if actual is above 99th percentile, score ~100)\n        combined = np.concatenate([actual_values, counterfactual_values])\n        percentile = stats.percentileofscore(combined, actual_mean)\n        anomaly_score = (percentile - 50) * 2  # Scale to [-100, 100]\n\n    return anomaly_score\n```\n\n### 7. Category-Level Counterfactual Analysis\n```python\ndef analyze_category_counterfactuals(df, date_col, value_col, category_col,\n                                     baseline_start, baseline_end,\n                                     treatment_start, treatment_end):\n    \"\"\"\n    Analyze counterfactual predictions for each category\n    \"\"\"\n    results = []\n\n    for category in df[category_col].unique():\n        cat_data = df[df[category_col] == category].copy()\n\n        # Aggregate to daily level\n        daily = cat_data.groupby(date_col)[value_col].sum().reset_index()\n        daily = daily.sort_values(date_col)\n\n        # Get baseline data for training\n        train_data = daily[(daily[date_col] >= baseline_start) &\n                          (daily[date_col] <= baseline_end)]\n\n        if len(train_data) < 7:  # Generate counterfactual prediction\n        # Using simple method: average of last N days before treatment\n        cf_prediction = train_data[value_col].tail(30).mean()\n\n        # Calculate anomaly score\n        actual_avg = treatment_data[value_col].mean()\n\n        anomaly_score = calculate_anomaly_score(\n            treatment_data[value_col].values,\n            np.full(len(treatment_data), cf_prediction)\n        )\n\n        results.append({\n            'category': category,\n            'baseline_avg': train_data[value_col].mean(),\n            'counterfactual_avg': cf_prediction,\n            'treatment_avg': actual_avg,\n            'anomaly_score': anomaly_score\n        })\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(results)\n```\n\n## Model Selection Guidelines\n\n| Situation | Recommended Method |\n|-----------|-------------------|\n| Limited data (<30 points) | Simple historical average |\n| Clear seasonality | Seasonal decomposition or Prophet |\n| Strong trend | Exponential smoothing or regression |\n| Complex patterns | Prophet (handles trend + seasonality) |\n| Need uncertainty bounds | Prophet or bootstrap methods |\n\n## Best Practices\n1. Data Preparation for Time Series\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\nfrom datetime import datetime\n\n# Aggregate to daily level\ndaily_sales = df.groupby(['date', 'category']).agg({\n    'amount': 'sum',\n    'quantity': 'sum',\n    'transaction_id': 'count'\n}).reset_index()\ndaily_sales.columns = ['date', 'category', 'total_sales', 'total_quantity', 'n_transactions']\n\n# Create complete date range\ndate_range = pd.date_range(start=df['date'].min(), end=df['date'].max(), freq='D')\n\n# Fill missing dates with zeros\ncomplete_ts = df.set_index('date').reindex(date_range).fillna(0)\n```\n\n### 2. Statistical Methods for Anomaly Detection\n\n#### Z-Score Method\n```python\nfrom scipy import stats\n\ndef detect_anomalies_zscore(series, threshold=3):\n    \"\"\"Detect anomalies using z-score method\"\"\"\n    z_scores = np.abs(stats.zscore(series))\n    return z_scores > threshold\n```\n\n#### Rolling Statistics\n```python\ndef detect_anomalies_rolling(series, window=30, n_std=2):\n    \"\"\"Detect anomalies using rolling mean and std\"\"\"\n    rolling_mean = series.rolling(window=window).mean()\n    rolling_std = series.rolling(window=window).std()\n\n    upper_bound = rolling_mean + n_std * rolling_std\n    lower_bound = rolling_mean - n_std * rolling_std\n\n    anomalies = (series > upper_bound) | (series < lower_bound)\n    return anomalies, upper_bound, lower_bound\n```\n\n### 3. Counterfactual Prediction\n\n#### Using Historical Average\n```python\ndef counterfactual_historical_avg(pre_period_data, post_period_length):\n    \"\"\"Predict counterfactual using historical average\"\"\"\n    return np.repeat(pre_period_data.mean(), post_period_length)\n```\n\n#### Using Same Period Last Year\n```python\ndef counterfactual_same_period_last_year(df, target_start, target_end):\n    \"\"\"Use same period from previous year as counterfactual\"\"\"\n    last_year_start = target_start - pd.DateOffset(years=1)\n    last_year_end = target_end - pd.DateOffset(years=1)\n    return df[(df['date'] >= last_year_start) & (df['date'] <= last_year_end)]\n```\n\n#### Using Prophet for Counterfactual\n```python\nfrom prophet import Prophet\n\ndef predict_counterfactual_prophet(df, pre_period_end, prediction_periods):\n    \"\"\"\n    Train on pre-period data and predict counterfactual for post-period\n    \"\"\"\n    # Prepare data for Prophet\n    train_df = df[df['ds'] <= pre_period_end][['ds', 'y']]\n\n    # Fit model\n    model = Prophet(yearly_seasonality=True, weekly_seasonality=True, daily_seasonality=False)\n    model.fit(train_df)\n\n    # Make future predictions\n    future = model.make_future_dataframe(periods=prediction_periods)\n    forecast = model.predict(future)\n\n    return forecast[['ds', 'yhat', 'yhat_lower', 'yhat_upper']]\n```\n\n### 4. Anomaly Index Calculation\n\n```python\ndef calculate_anomaly_index(actual, predicted, method='normalized'):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate anomaly index ranging from -100 to 100\n\n    100: Maximum positive anomaly (surge)\n    0: No anomaly (actual = predicted)\n    -100: Maximum negative anomaly (slump)\n    \"\"\"\n    if method == 'normalized':\n        # Calculate percentage deviation\n        deviation = (actual - predicted) / (predicted + 1e-10)  # avoid division by zero\n\n        # Normalize to [-100, 100] using tanh\n        anomaly_index = np.tanh(deviation) * 100\n\n    elif method == 'zscore':\n        # Use z-score based approach\n        combined = np.concatenate([actual, predicted])\n        std = np.std(combined) if np.std(combined) > 0 else 1\n        z_score = (actual.mean() - predicted.mean()) / std\n        anomaly_index = np.tanh(z_score) * 100\n\n    return anomaly_index\n```\n\n### 5. Category-Level Anomaly Detection\n\n```python\ndef analyze_category_anomalies(df, baseline_start, baseline_end,\n                               treatment_start, treatment_end):\n    \"\"\"\n    Analyze anomalies for each product category\n    \"\"\"\n    results = []\n\n    for category in df['category'].unique():\n        cat_data = df[df['category'] == category].copy()\n\n        # Get baseline period data (all historical data before treatment)\n        baseline = cat_data[(cat_data['date'] >= baseline_start) &\n                           (cat_data['date'] <= baseline_end)]\n\n        # Get treatment period data\n        treatment = cat_data[(cat_data['date'] >= treatment_start) &\n                            (cat_data['date'] <= treatment_end)]\n\n        if len(baseline) > 0 and len(treatment) > 0:\n            # Calculate predicted (counterfactual) from baseline\n            predicted_daily_avg = baseline['sales'].mean()\n            actual_daily_avg = treatment['sales'].mean()\n\n            # Calculate anomaly index\n            anomaly_idx = calculate_anomaly_index(\n                np.array([actual_daily_avg]),\n                np.array([predicted_daily_avg])\n            )\n\n            results.append({\n                'category': category,\n                'anomaly_index': anomaly_idx,\n                'baseline_avg': predicted_daily_avg,\n                'treatment_avg': actual_daily_avg\n            })\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(results)\n```\n\n### 6. Identifying Surges and Slumps\n\n```python\ndef identify_surges_slumps(anomaly_df, n_top=10):\n    \"\"\"\n    Identify top surge and slump categories\n    \"\"\"\n    # Sort by anomaly index\n    sorted_df = anomaly_df.sort_values('anomaly_index', ascending=False)\n\n    # Top surges (highest positive anomaly)\n    surges = sorted_df.head(n_top)\n\n    # Top slumps (lowest negative anomaly)\n    slumps = sorted_df.tail(n_top)\n\n    return surges, slumps\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n1. Use sufficient historical data for reliable predictions\n2. Account for seasonality (weekly, monthly, yearly patterns)\n3. Consider multiple baseline periods for robust estimates\n4. Validate predictions on held-out data before applying to treatment period\n5. Document anomaly thresholds and methodology\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Binary Feature Extraction from Yes/No Questions\n```python\ndef encode_binary_response(series, yes_values=['Yes', 'yes', 'Y', 'y', '1', True]):\n    \"\"\"Convert yes/no responses to binary 0/1\"\"\"\n    return series.isin(yes_values).astype(int)\n\n# Apply to multiple columns\nbinary_cols = ['Q-personal-diabetes', 'Q-personal-wheelchair']\nfor col in binary_cols:\n    new_col = col.replace('Q-', 'has_').replace('-', '_')\n    df[new_col] = encode_binary_response(df[col])\n```\n\n### 4. Handling Multi-Select Responses\n```python\ndef encode_multiselect(series, separator=','):\n    \"\"\"Encode multi-select responses into binary columns\"\"\"\n    # Split and get unique values\n    all_values = series.str.split(separator).explode().str.strip().unique()\n    all_values = [v for v in all_values if pd.notna(v)]\n\n    # Create binary columns for each value\n    result = pd.DataFrame()\n    for value in all_values:\n        col_name = f\"has_{value.replace(' ', '_').lower()}\"\n        result[col_name] = series.str.contains(value, na=False).astype(int)\n\n    return result\n```\n\n### 5. Demographic Feature Engineering\n\n#### Age Group Features\n```python\ndef create_age_features(df, age_col):\n    \"\"\"Create derived age features\"\"\"\n    # Parse age ranges and extract midpoint\n    age_map = {\n        '18 - 24 years': 21,\n        '25 - 34 years': 29.5,\n        '35 - 44 years': 39.5,\n        '45 - 54 years': 49.5,\n        '55 - 64 years': 59.5,\n        '65 and older': 70\n    }\n    df['age_midpoint'] = df[age_col].map(age_map)\n\n    # Create generational groups\n    gen_map = {\n        '18 - 24 years': 'GenZ',\n        '25 - 34 years': 'Millennial',\n        '35 - 44 years': 'Millennial',\n        '45 - 54 years': 'GenX',\n        '55 - 64 years': 'Boomer',\n        '65 and older': 'Boomer'\n    }\n    df['generation'] = df[age_col].map(gen_map)\n\n    return df\n```\n\n#### Income Features\n```python\ndef create_income_features(df, income_col):\n    \"\"\"Create derived income features\"\"\"\n    # Map to numeric midpoints\n    income_map = {\n        'Less than $25,000': 12500,\n        '$25,000 - $49,999': 37500,\n        '$50,000 - $74,999': 62500,\n        '$75,000 - $99,999': 87500,\n        '$100,000 - $149,999': 125000,\n        '$150,000 or more': 175000,\n        'Prefer not to say': None\n    }\n    df['income_midpoint'] = df[income_col].map(income_map)\n\n    # Create income brackets\n    def income_bracket(val):\n        if val in ['Less than $25,000']:\n            return 'low_income'\n        elif val in ['$25,000 - $49,999', '$50,000 - $74,999']:\n            return 'middle_income'\n        else:\n            return 'high_income'\n\n    df['income_bracket'] = df[income_col].apply(income_bracket)\n\n    return df\n```\n\n#### Household Size Features\n```python\ndef create_household_features(df, hh_col, users_col):\n    \"\"\"Create household-related features\"\"\"\n    # Parse numeric values\n    hh_map = {'1 (just me!)': 1, '2': 2, '3': 3, '4+': 4}\n    df['household_size'] = df[hh_col].map(hh_map)\n    df['amazon_users'] = df[users_col].map(hh_map)\n\n    # Create ratios\n    df['amazon_penetration'] = df['amazon_users'] / df['household_size']\n\n    # Create binary flags\n    df['is_single_household'] = (df['household_size'] == 1).astype(int)\n    df['is_large_household'] = (df['household_size'] >= 3).astype(int)\n\n    return df\n```\n\n### 6. Interaction Features\n```python\ndef create_interaction_features(df, col1, col2, prefix='interaction'):\n    \"\"\"Create interaction features between two categorical columns\"\"\"\n    df[f'{prefix}_{col1}_{col2}'] = df[col1].astype(str) + '_' + df[col2].astype(str)\n\n    # One-hot encode the interaction\n    interaction_dummies = pd.get_dummies(\n        df[f'{prefix}_{col1}_{col2}'],\n        prefix=f'{prefix}'\n    )\n    return pd.concat([df, interaction_dummies], axis=1)\n```\n\n### 7. Frequency Encoding\n```python\ndef frequency_encode(df, col):\n    \"\"\"Encode categories by their frequency\"\"\"\n    freq = df[col].value_counts(normalize=True)\n    df[f'{col}_freq'] = df[col].map(freq)\n    return df\n```\n\n### 8. Create derived features\n    df = create_age_features(df, 'Q-demos-age')\n    df = create_income_features(df, 'Q-demos-income')\n\n    return df\n```\n\n## Best Practices\n1. Data Preparation for DiD\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef prepare_did_data(purchases, demographics, baseline_start, baseline_end,\n                     treatment_start, treatment_end, categories):\n    \"\"\"\n    Prepare data for DiD analysis\n    \"\"\"\n    # Filter purchases to relevant categories and time periods\n    purchases = purchases[purchases['category'].isin(categories)]\n\n    # Create period indicator\n    purchases['period'] = np.where(\n        (purchases['date'] >= treatment_start) & (purchases['date'] <= treatment_end),\n        'treatment', 'baseline'\n    )\n    purchases = purchases[purchases['period'].isin(['treatment', 'baseline'])]\n\n    # Aggregate by user, category, period\n    agg_intensive = purchases.groupby(['user_id', 'category', 'period']).agg({\n        'amount': 'sum'\n    }).reset_index()\n    agg_intensive.columns = ['user_id', 'category', 'period', 'total_spend']\n\n    # For extensive margin, need to track who purchased\n    agg_extensive = purchases.groupby(['user_id', 'category', 'period']).size().reset_index()\n    agg_extensive.columns = ['user_id', 'category', 'period', 'n_purchases']\n    agg_extensive['has_purchase'] = 1\n\n    # Merge with demographics\n    agg_intensive = agg_intensive.merge(demographics, on='user_id')\n    agg_extensive = agg_extensive.merge(demographics, on='user_id')\n\n    return agg_intensive, agg_extensive\n```\n\n### 2. Univariate DiD for Intensive Margin\n```python\nimport statsmodels.api as sm\nfrom scipy import stats\n\ndef univariate_did_intensive(df, feature_col, spend_col='total_spend', period_col='period'):\n    \"\"\"\n    Run univariate DiD analysis for intensive margin\n\n    For each feature value, estimate how the treatment effect differs.\n    \"\"\"\n    results = []\n\n    # Get unique feature values\n    feature_values = df[feature_col].unique()\n\n    for feature_val in feature_values:\n        # Subset to this feature value\n        subset = df[df[feature_col] == feature_val]\n\n        # Calculate means\n        baseline_mean = subset[subset[period_col] == 'baseline'][spend_col].mean()\n        treatment_mean = subset[subset[period_col] == 'treatment'][spend_col].mean()\n\n        # DiD for this subgroup\n        did_estimate = treatment_mean - baseline_mean\n\n        results.append({\n            'feature': feature_col,\n            'feature_value': feature_val,\n            'baseline_mean': baseline_mean,\n            'treatment_mean': treatment_mean,\n            'did_estimate': did_estimate\n        })\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(results)\n\n\ndef did_with_interaction(df, feature_col, spend_col='total_spend'):\n    \"\"\"\n    DiD regression with feature interaction to identify heterogeneous effects\n\n    Y = beta0 + beta1*Post + beta2*Feature + beta3*(Post*Feature) + epsilon\n\n    beta3 captures the heterogeneous treatment effect\n    \"\"\"\n    # Create post period indicator (1 for treatment, 0 for baseline)\n    df = df.copy()\n    df['post'] = (df['period'] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n\n    # For binary features\n    if df[feature_col].nunique() == 2:\n        df['feature'] = df[feature_col].astype(int)\n    else:\n        # For categorical, use dummy coding\n        df['feature'] = pd.Categorical(df[feature_col]).codes\n\n    # Create interaction term\n    df['post_x_feature'] = df['post'] * df['feature']\n\n    # Run OLS regression\n    X = sm.add_constant(df[['post', 'feature', 'post_x_feature']])\n    y = df[spend_col]\n\n    model = sm.OLS(y, X).fit(cov_type='HC3')  # Robust standard errors\n\n    return {\n        'feature': feature_col,\n        'did_estimate': model.params['post_x_feature'],\n        'p_value': model.pvalues['post_x_feature'],\n        'std_err': model.bse['post_x_feature'],\n        'conf_int': model.conf_int().loc['post_x_feature'].tolist()\n    }\n```\n\n### 3. Extensive Margin DiD (Probability of Purchase)\n```python\ndef extensive_margin_did(df, feature_col, all_users_df, categories):\n    \"\"\"\n    Analyze extensive margin: probability of purchase\n\n    Need to include non-purchasers in the analysis\n    \"\"\"\n    results = []\n\n    for category in categories:\n        # Get all users who could have purchased\n        all_users = all_users_df.copy()\n\n        # Get purchasers for this category\n        cat_purchasers = df[df['category'] == category]\n\n        for period in ['baseline', 'treatment']:\n            period_purchasers = cat_purchasers[cat_purchasers['period'] == period]\n\n            # Create full panel with all users\n            all_users[f'purchased_{period}'] = all_users['user_id'].isin(\n                period_purchasers['user_id']\n            ).astype(int)\n\n        # Calculate purchase rates by feature\n        for feature_val in all_users[feature_col].unique():\n            subset = all_users[all_users[feature_col] == feature_val]\n\n            baseline_rate = subset['purchased_baseline'].mean()\n            treatment_rate = subset['purchased_treatment'].mean()\n\n            did_estimate = treatment_rate - baseline_rate\n\n            results.append({\n                'category': category,\n                'feature': feature_col,\n                'feature_value': feature_val,\n                'baseline_rate': baseline_rate,\n                'treatment_rate': treatment_rate,\n                'did_estimate': did_estimate\n            })\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(results)\n```\n\n### 4. Multivariate DiD with Heterogeneous Effects\n```python\ndef multivariate_did(df, feature_cols, outcome_col, is_binary_outcome=False):\n    \"\"\"\n    Multivariate DiD with multiple feature interactions\n\n    Y = beta0 + beta1*Post + sum(beta_k*Feature_k) + sum(gamma_k*Post*Feature_k) + epsilon\n    \"\"\"\n    df = df.copy()\n    df['post'] = (df['period'] == 'treatment').astype(int)\n\n    # Prepare features and interactions\n    X_vars = ['post']\n    for col in feature_cols:\n        X_vars.append(col)\n        interaction_col = f'post_x_{col}'\n        df[interaction_col] = df['post'] * df[col]\n        X_vars.append(interaction_col)\n\n    X = sm.add_constant(df[X_vars])\n    y = df[outcome_col]\n\n    if is_binary_outcome:\n        # Logistic regression for extensive margin\n        model = sm.Logit(y, X).fit(disp=0)\n    else:\n        # OLS for intensive margin\n        model = sm.OLS(y, X).fit(cov_type='HC3')\n\n    # Extract heterogeneous effects\n    results = []\n    for col in feature_cols:\n        interaction_col = f'post_x_{col}'\n        if interaction_col in model.params.index:\n            results.append({\n                'feature': col,\n                'did_estimate': model.params[interaction_col],\n                'p_value': model.pvalues[interaction_col],\n                'std_err': model.bse[interaction_col]\n            })\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(results), model\n```\n\n### 5. Identifying Top Drivers\n```python\ndef identify_top_drivers(did_results, n_top=3, margin_type='intensive'):\n    \"\"\"\n    Identify top features driving anomalies\n\n    For surges: rank by positive DiD estimates\n    For slumps: rank by negative DiD estimates\n    \"\"\"\n    # Sort by absolute DiD magnitude\n    did_results = did_results.copy()\n    did_results['abs_did'] = did_results['did_estimate'].abs()\n\n    # For surge categories (positive anomaly), want largest positive effects\n    surge_drivers = did_results.nlargest(n_top, 'did_estimate')\n\n    # For slump categories (negative anomaly), want largest negative effects\n    slump_drivers = did_results.nsmallest(n_top, 'did_estimate')\n\n    return surge_drivers, slump_drivers\n```\n\n### 6. Complete Analysis Pipeline\n```python\ndef run_causal_analysis(purchases, demographics, categories,\n                        baseline_period, treatment_period, feature_cols):\n    \"\"\"\n    Complete DiD analysis pipeline\n    \"\"\"\n    results = {\n        'surge_categories': [],\n        'slump_categories': []\n    }\n\n    for category in categories:\n        cat_results = {\n            'category': category,\n            'intensive_margin': [],\n            'extensive_margin': []\n        }\n\n        # Filter to category\n        cat_data = purchases[purchases['category'] == category]\n\n        # Intensive margin analysis\n        intensive_data = prepare_intensive_margin_data(cat_data, demographics)\n        for feature in feature_cols:\n            did_result = did_with_interaction(intensive_data, feature)\n            cat_results['intensive_margin'].append(did_result)\n\n        # Extensive margin analysis\n        extensive_data = prepare_extensive_margin_data(\n            cat_data, demographics, baseline_period, treatment_period\n        )\n        for feature in feature_cols:\n            did_result = extensive_margin_did_feature(extensive_data, feature)\n            cat_results['extensive_margin'].append(did_result)\n\n        # Sort and get top 3 drivers\n        cat_results['intensive_margin'] = sorted(\n            cat_results['intensive_margin'],\n            key=lambda x: abs(x['did_estimate']),\n            reverse=True\n        )[:3]\n\n        cat_results['extensive_margin'] = sorted(\n            cat_results['extensive_margin'],\n            key=lambda x: abs(x['did_estimate']),\n            reverse=True\n        )[:3]\n\n        results['surge_categories'].append(cat_results)\n\n    return results\n```\n\n## Assumptions and Validation\n1. **Parallel Trends**: Treatment and control groups would have followed similar trends without treatment\n2. **No Spillovers**: Treatment of one group doesn't affect outcomes of other groups\n3. **Stable Composition**: Group composition remains stable across periods\n\n## Best Practices\n1. Validate parallel trends assumption using pre-treatment data\n2. Use robust standard errors for inference\n3. Consider multiple comparison corrections when testing many features\n4. Report effect sizes along with statistical significance\n5. Interpret effects in context of baseline means\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

Simple Historical Average Approach\n```python\nimport pandas as pd\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef counterfactual_simple_avg(historical_data, target_period_length):\n    \"\"\"\n    Use simple historical average as counterfactual prediction\n    \"\"\"\n    avg_daily = historical_data.mean()\n    return np.full(target_period_length, avg_daily)\n```\n\n### 2. Same Period Prior Year\n```python\ndef counterfactual_prior_year(df, date_col, value_col,\n                               target_start, target_end):\n    \"\"\"\n    Use same calendar period from previous year\n    \"\"\"\n    # Calculate dates for previous year\n    prior_start = target_start - pd.DateOffset(years=1)\n    prior_end = target_end - pd.DateOffset(years=1)\n\n    # Get prior year data\n    prior_data = df[(df[date_col] >= prior_start) &\n                    (df[date_col] <= prior_end)][value_col]\n\n    return prior_data.values\n```\n\n### 3. Seasonal Decomposition + Trend Extrapolation\n```python\nfrom statsmodels.tsa.seasonal import seasonal_decompose\nfrom statsmodels.tsa.holtwinters import ExponentialSmoothing\n\ndef counterfactual_seasonal_decomposition(series, periods_ahead, freq=7):\n    \"\"\"\n    Decompose time series and extrapolate trend + seasonality\n    \"\"\"\n    # Decompose\n    decomposition = seasonal_decompose(series, model='additive', period=freq)\n\n    # Extrapolate trend using exponential smoothing\n    trend = decomposition.trend.dropna()\n    trend_model = ExponentialSmoothing(trend, trend='add').fit()\n    trend_forecast = trend_model.forecast(periods_ahead)\n\n    # Get seasonal pattern\n    seasonal = decomposition.seasonal\n    seasonal_pattern = seasonal.iloc[:freq].values\n\n    # Combine\n    n_periods = periods_ahead\n    seasonal_forecast = np.tile(seasonal_pattern, n_periods // freq + 1)[:n_periods]\n\n    counterfactual = trend_forecast.values + seasonal_forecast\n\n    return counterfactual\n```\n\n### 4. Prophet-Based Counterfactual\n```python\nfrom prophet import Prophet\n\ndef counterfactual_prophet(df, date_col, value_col,\n                           train_end, forecast_days):\n    \"\"\"\n    Use Prophet for sophisticated counterfactual prediction\n    \"\"\"\n    # Prepare data for Prophet\n    prophet_df = df[[date_col, value_col]].copy()\n    prophet_df.columns = ['ds', 'y']\n    prophet_df = prophet_df[prophet_df['ds'] <= train_end]\n\n    # Fit model\n    model = Prophet(\n        yearly_seasonality=True,\n        weekly_seasonality=True,\n        daily_seasonality=False,\n        changepoint_prior_scale=0.05  # Control flexibility\n    )\n    model.fit(prophet_df)\n\n    # Create future dataframe\n    future = model.make_future_dataframe(periods=forecast_days)\n    forecast = model.predict(future)\n\n    # Get counterfactual predictions\n    counterfactual = forecast[forecast['ds'] > train_end][['ds', 'yhat', 'yhat_lower', 'yhat_upper']]\n\n    return counterfactual\n```\n\n### 5. Regression-Based Counterfactual\n```python\nimport statsmodels.api as sm\n\ndef counterfactual_regression(df, date_col, value_col,\n                              train_end, target_dates):\n    \"\"\"\n    Use regression with time features for counterfactual\n    \"\"\"\n    df = df.copy()\n\n    # Create time features\n    df['day_of_week'] = pd.to_datetime(df[date_col]).dt.dayofweek\n    df['month'] = pd.to_datetime(df[date_col]).dt.month\n    df['trend'] = range(len(df))\n\n    # Split data\n    train = df[df[date_col] <= train_end]\n\n    # Create dummies for categorical features\n    X_train = pd.get_dummies(train[['trend', 'day_of_week', 'month']],\n                             columns=['day_of_week', 'month'])\n    X_train = sm.add_constant(X_train)\n    y_train = train[value_col]\n\n    # Fit model\n    model = sm.OLS(y_train, X_train).fit()\n\n    # Prepare prediction features\n    pred_df = pd.DataFrame({'date': target_dates})\n    pred_df['day_of_week'] = pred_df['date'].dt.dayofweek\n    pred_df['month'] = pred_df['date'].dt.month\n    pred_df['trend'] = range(len(train), len(train) + len(target_dates))\n\n    X_pred = pd.get_dummies(pred_df[['trend', 'day_of_week', 'month']],\n                            columns=['day_of_week', 'month'])\n    X_pred = sm.add_constant(X_pred)\n\n    # Ensure same columns\n    for col in X_train.columns:\n        if col not in X_pred.columns:\n            X_pred[col] = 0\n    X_pred = X_pred[X_train.columns]\n\n    # Predict\n    counterfactual = model.predict(X_pred)\n\n    return counterfactual\n```\n\n### 6. Anomaly Score Calculation\n```python\ndef calculate_anomaly_score(actual_values, counterfactual_values,\n                            method='tanh_normalized', scale=1.0):\n    \"\"\"\n    Calculate anomaly score ranging from -100 to 100\n\n    Parameters:\n    - actual_values: observed values during treatment period\n    - counterfactual_values: predicted values (what should have happened)\n    - method: scoring method\n    - scale: scaling factor for sensitivity\n\n    Returns:\n    - anomaly_score: value in [-100, 100]\n      - 100: maximum surge (actual >> counterfactual)\n      - 0: no anomaly (actual ≈ counterfactual)\n      - -100: maximum slump (actual << counterfactual)\n    \"\"\"\n    actual_mean = np.mean(actual_values)\n    cf_mean = np.mean(counterfactual_values)\n\n    if method == 'tanh_normalized':\n        # Calculate relative deviation\n        if cf_mean > 0:\n            relative_dev = (actual_mean - cf_mean) / cf_mean\n        else:\n            relative_dev = actual_mean - cf_mean\n\n        # Apply tanh for bounded output\n        anomaly_score = np.tanh(relative_dev * scale) * 100\n\n    elif method == 'zscore':\n        # Z-score based approach\n        cf_std = np.std(counterfactual_values)\n        if cf_std > 0:\n            z = (actual_mean - cf_mean) / cf_std\n        else:\n            z = 0\n        anomaly_score = np.tanh(z * scale) * 100\n\n    elif method == 'percentile':\n        # Percentile-based (if actual is above 99th percentile, score ~100)\n        combined = np.concatenate([actual_values, counterfactual_values])\n        percentile = stats.percentileofscore(combined, actual_mean)\n        anomaly_score = (percentile - 50) * 2  # Scale to [-100, 100]\n\n    return anomaly_score\n```\n\n### 7. Category-Level Counterfactual Analysis\n```python\ndef analyze_category_counterfactuals(df, date_col, value_col, category_col,\n                                     baseline_start, baseline_end,\n                                     treatment_start, treatment_end):\n    \"\"\"\n    Analyze counterfactual predictions for each category\n    \"\"\"\n    results = []\n\n    for category in df[category_col].unique():\n        cat_data = df[df[category_col] == category].copy()\n\n        # Aggregate to daily level\n        daily = cat_data.groupby(date_col)[value_col].sum().reset_index()\n        daily = daily.sort_values(date_col)\n\n        # Get baseline data for training\n        train_data = daily[(daily[date_col] >= baseline_start) &\n                          (daily[date_col] <= baseline_end)]\n\n        if len(train_data) < 7:  # Need minimum data\n            continue\n\n        # Get treatment period for comparison\n        treatment_data = daily[(daily[date_col] >= treatment_start) &\n                              (daily[date_col] <= treatment_end)]\n\n        if len(treatment_data) == 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Generate counterfactual prediction\n        # Using simple method: average of last N days before treatment\n        cf_prediction = train_data[value_col].tail(30).mean()\n\n        # Calculate anomaly score\n        actual_avg = treatment_data[value_col].mean()\n\n        anomaly_score = calculate_anomaly_score(\n            treatment_data[value_col].values,\n            np.full(len(treatment_data), cf_prediction)\n        )\n\n        results.append({\n            'category': category,\n            'baseline_avg': train_data[value_col].mean(),\n            'counterfactual_avg': cf_prediction,\n            'treatment_avg': actual_avg,\n            'anomaly_score': anomaly_score\n        })\n\n    return pd.DataFrame(results)\n```\n\n## Model Selection Guidelines\n\n| Situation | Recommended Method |\n|-----------|-------------------|\n| Limited data (<30 points) | Simple historical average |\n| Clear seasonality | Seasonal decomposition or Prophet |\n| Strong trend | Exponential smoothing or regression |\n| Complex patterns | Prophet (handles trend + seasonality) |\n| Need uncertainty bounds | Prophet or bootstrap methods |\n\n## Best Practices\n1. Use data from the same day-of-week pattern when possible\n2. Account for holidays and special events\n3. Validate counterfactual model on held-out pre-treatment data\n4. Consider multiple counterfactual methods and compare\n5. Report uncertainty bounds around counterfactual predictions\n6. Document assumptions about what \"would have happened\"\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

\"\"\"\n    if counterfactual_mean > 0:\n        relative_dev = (actual_mean - counterfactual_mean) / counterfactual_mean\n    else:\n        relative_dev = actual_mean if actual_mean != 0 else 0\n\n feature_col,\n            'did_estimate': round(float(model.params.get('post_x_feature', 0)), 4),\n            'p_value': round(float(model.pvalues.get('post_x_feature', 1)), 4),\n            'method': 'Univariate DiD'\n        }\n    except:\n        return None\n\ndef run_univariate_did_extensive(df, feature_col, category):\n    \"\"\"Run univariate DiD for extensive margin\"\"\"\n
